### File 2: `batch_excel_template.py` - Excel Template Generator

```python
"""
Generate Excel template for batch bridge processing
"""

import pandas as pd
from openpyxl import Workbook
from openpyxl.styles import Font, PatternFill, Alignment, Border, Side
from openpyxl.utils.dataframe import dataframe_to_rows


def create_batch_excel_template(output_path: str = "bridges_master_template.xlsx"):
    """
    Create Excel template for batch bridge processing
    """
    
    # Create workbook
    wb = Workbook()
    
    # Remove default sheet
    wb.remove(wb.active)
    
    # ===== Sheet 1: BRIDGE_LIST =====
    ws_list = wb.create_sheet("BRIDGE_LIST")
    
    # Header data
    headers = [
        'bridge_id', 'bridge_name', 'chainage',
        'span_lengths', 'deck_width', 'skew_angle', 'skew_direction',
        'superstructure_type', 'deck_thickness', 'wearing_coat',
        'pier_type', 'pier_width', 'pier_height',
        'abutment_type',
        'foundation_type', 'pile_diameter', 'pile_length',
        'num_piles_long', 'num_piles_trans',
        'generate_plan', 'generate_long_section',
        'generate_cross_section', 'generate_foundation', 'generate_reinforcement'
    ]
    
    # Sample data
    sample_data = [
        [
            'BRG-001', 'Bridge 1', 10.000,
            '25,30,25', 12.0, 15.0, 'RIGHT',
            'T-BEAM', 0.25, 0.065,
            'WALL', 2.0, 10.0,
            'CANTILEVER',
            'PILE', 1.2, 15.0,
            3, 3,
            'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'TRUE'
        ],
        [
            'BRG-002', 'Bridge 2', 25.500,
            '20,20', 9.0, 0.0, 'NONE',
            'SOLID_SLAB', 0.50, 0.065,
            'WALL', 1.5, 8.0,
            'CANTILEVER',
            'WELL', 6.0, 12.0,
            1, 1,
            'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'FALSE'
        ],
        [
            'BRG-003', 'Bridge 3', 45.000,
            '30', 7.5, 30.0, 'LEFT',
            'T-BEAM', 0.25, 0.065,
            'WALL', 2.0, 12.0,
            'CANTILEVER',
            'PILE', 1.0, 12.0,
            2, 2,
            'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'TRUE', 'TRUE'
        ]
    ]
    
    # Create DataFrame
    df = pd.DataFrame(sample_data, columns=headers)
    
    # Write to worksheet
    for r_idx, row in enumerate(dataframe_to_rows(df, index=False, header=True), 1):
        for c_idx, value in enumerate(row, 1):
            cell = ws_list.cell(row=r_idx, column=c_idx, value=value)
            
            # Header styling
            if r_idx == 1:
                cell.font = Font(bold=True, color="FFFFFF")
                cell.fill = PatternFill(start_color="366092", end_color="366092", fill_type="solid")
                cell.alignment = Alignment(horizontal="center", vertical="center", wrap_text=True)
            else:
                cell.alignment = Alignment(horizontal="center", vertical="center")
            
            # Border
            cell.border = Border(
                left=Side(style='thin'),
                right=Side(style='thin'),
                top=Side(style='thin'),
                bottom=Side(style='thin')
            )
    
    # Adjust column widths
    for col in ws_list.columns:
        max_length = 0
        column = col[0].column_letter
        for cell in col:
            try:
                if len(str(cell.value)) > max_length:
                    max_length = len(str(cell.value))
            except:
                pass
        adjusted_width = min(max_length + 2, 30)
        ws_list.column_dimensions[column].width = adjusted_width
    
    # Add instructions
    ws_list['A30'] = "INSTRUCTIONS:"
    ws_list['A30'].font = Font(bold=True, size=12)
    
    instructions = [
        "1. bridge_id: Unique identifier for each bridge (e.g., BRG-001)",
        "2. span_lengths: Comma-separated span lengths in meters (e.g., '25,30,25')",
        "3. skew_direction: 'LEFT', 'RIGHT', or 'NONE'",
        "4. superstructure_type: 'T-BEAM', 'I-GIRDER', 'BOX-GIRDER', 'SOLID_SLAB'",
        "5. foundation_type: 'PILE', 'WELL', or 'OPEN'",
        "6. generate_* columns: Use 'TRUE' or 'FALSE' to control output",
        "7. Add more rows below for additional bridges",
        "8. Do not modify column headers"
    ]
    
    for i, instruction in enumerate(instructions, 31):
        ws_list[f'A{i}'] = instruction
    
    # ===== Sheet 2: STANDARD_COMPONENTS =====
    ws_std = wb.create_sheet("STANDARD_COMPONENTS")
    
    std_data = {
        'Component': [
            'Standard Pier', 'Standard Abutment', 'Standard Pile',
            'Standard Bearing', 'Standard Parapet'
        ],
        'Type': [
            'WALL', 'CANTILEVER', 'BORED_CAST_IN_SITU',
            'ELASTOMERIC', 'RCC_CRASH_BARRIER'
        ],
        'Dimensions': [
            '2.0m x 10.0m', '1.5m x 11.0m', '1.2m dia x 15m',
            '300x400x50mm', '0.9m high'
        ],
        'Default': ['YES', 'YES', 'YES', 'YES', 'YES']
    }
    
    df_std = pd.DataFrame(std_data)
    
    for r_idx, row in enumerate(dataframe_to_rows(df_std, index=False, header=True), 1):
        for c_idx, value in enumerate(row, 1):
            cell = ws_std.cell(row=r_idx, column=c_idx, value=value)
            if r_idx == 1:
                cell.font = Font(bold=True, color="FFFFFF")
                cell.fill = PatternFill(start_color="366092", end_color="366092", fill_type="solid")
    
    # ===== Sheet 3: TEMPLATES =====
    ws_temp = wb.create_sheet("TEMPLATES")
    
    templates = {
        'Template_Name': [
            'Minor_Bridge_2Lane',
            'Major_Bridge_2Lane',
            'Major_Bridge_4Lane',
            'Viaduct_Standard'
        ],
        'Description': [
            'Minor bridge up to 30m total length',
            'Major bridge 2-lane configuration',
            'Major bridge 4-lane configuration',
            'Standard viaduct configuration'
        ],
        'Deck_Width': [9.0, 12.0, 15.0, 13.5],
        'Girder_Type': ['T-BEAM', 'T-BEAM', 'I-GIRDER', 'BOX-GIRDER'],
        'Foundation': ['OPEN', 'PILE', 'PILE', 'PILE']
    }
    
    df_temp = pd.DataFrame(templates)
    
    for r_idx, row in enumerate(dataframe_to_rows(df_temp, index=False, header=True), 1):
        for c_idx, value in enumerate(row, 1):
            cell = ws_temp.cell(row=r_idx, column=c_idx, value=value)
            if r_idx == 1:
                cell.font = Font(bold=True, color="FFFFFF")
                cell.fill = PatternFill(start_color="366092", end_color="366092", fill_type="solid")
    
    # ===== Sheet 4: OUTPUT_SETTINGS =====
    ws_settings = wb.create_sheet("OUTPUT_SETTINGS")
    
    settings = [
        ['Setting', 'Value', 'Description'],
        ['Drawing_Scale', '1:100', 'Default drawing scale'],
        ['Sheet_Size', 'A1', 'Default sheet size (A0/A1/A2)'],
        ['AutoCAD_Version', '2018', 'DXF version'],
        ['Generate_PDF', 'FALSE', 'Also generate PDF output'],
        ['Organize_By_Chainage', 'TRUE', 'Organize folders by chainage'],
        ['Parallel_Processing', 'TRUE', 'Use multiple CPU cores'],
        ['Max_Workers', '4', 'Number of parallel processes']
    ]
    
    for r_idx, row in enumerate(settings, 1):
        for c_idx, value in enumerate(row, 1):
            cell = ws_settings.cell(row=r_idx, column=c_idx, value=value)
            if r_idx == 1:
                cell.font = Font(bold=True, color="FFFFFF")
                cell.fill = PatternFill(start_color="366092", end_color="366092", fill_type="solid")
    
    # Save workbook
    wb.save(output_path)
    print(f"Excel template created: {output_path}")
    print(f"Sheets: {wb.sheetnames}")
    
    return output_path


def create_sample_batch_file(num_bridges: int = 100, output_path: str = "sample_100_bridges.xlsx"):
    """
    Create a sample Excel file with multiple bridges for testing
    """
    import random
    
    data = []
    
    for i in range(1, num_bridges + 1):
        chainage = i * 2.5  # Every 2.5 km
        
        # Random spans
        num_spans = random.choice([1, 2, 3, 4])
        span_lengths = [random.choice([20, 25, 30]) for _ in range(num_spans)]
        span_str = ','.join(map(str, span_lengths))
        
        # Random width
        width = random.choice([7.5, 9.0, 12.0])
        
        # Random skew
        skew = random.choice([0, 0, 0, 15, 30])  # Mostly straight
        
        data.append({
            'bridge_id': f'BRG-{i:03d}',
            'bridge_name': f'Bridge {i}',
            'chainage': chainage,
            'span_lengths': span_str,
            'deck_width': width,
            'skew_angle': skew,
            'skew_direction': random.choice(['LEFT', 'RIGHT', 'NONE']),
            'superstructure_type': 'T-BEAM',
            'deck_thickness': 0.25,
            'wearing_coat': 0.065,
            'pier_type': 'WALL',
            'pier_width': 2.0,
            'pier_height': 10.0,
            'abutment_type': 'CANTILEVER',
            'foundation_type': 'PILE',
            'pile_diameter': 1.2,
            'pile_length': 15.0,
            'num_piles_long': 3,
            'num_piles_trans': 3,
            'generate_plan': 'TRUE',
            'generate_long_section': 'TRUE',
            'generate_cross_section': 'TRUE',
            'generate_foundation': 'TRUE',
            'generate_reinforcement': 'TRUE'
        })
    
    df = pd.DataFrame(data)
    df.to_excel(output_path, sheet_name='BRIDGE_LIST', index=False)
    
    print(f"Sample batch file created: {output_path}")
    print(f"Bridges: {num_bridges}")
    print(f"Chainage range: 2.5 - {num_bridges * 2.5} km")
    
    return output_path


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Create template
    create_batch_excel_template("bridges_master_template.xlsx")
    
    # Create sample with 100 bridges
    create_sample_batch_file(100, "sample_100_bridges.xlsx")
```

### File 3: `example_batch_processing.py` - Complete Example

```python
"""
Example: Batch process multiple bridges
"""

import os
import time
from batch_processor_core import (
    BatchProcessor, BatchConfig, BridgeInputData,
    ProcessingStatus
)
from batch_excel_template import create_sample_batch_file


def example_batch_process_10_bridges():
    """Process 10 sample bridges"""
    print("=" * 80)
    print("BATCH PROCESSING EXAMPLE - 10 BRIDGES")
    print("=" * 80)
    
    # Create sample Excel file
    excel_file = create_sample_batch_file(10, "demo_10_bridges.xlsx")
    
    # Create output directory
    output_dir = "output_batch_10_bridges"
    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    
    # Create config
    config = BatchConfig(
        input_excel_path=excel_file,
        output_base_directory=output_dir,
        max_workers=4,
        use_parallel=True,
        validate_before_process=True,
        organize_by_chainage=True
    )
    
    # Run processor
    processor = BatchProcessor(config)
    processor.run()
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("RESULTS")
    print("=" * 80)
    
    for result in processor.results:
        status_icon = "✓" if result.status == ProcessingStatus.COMPLETED else "✗"
        print(f"{status_icon} {result.bridge_id}: {result.status.value}")
        if result.output_files:
            print(f"   Files: {len(result.output_files)}")
            for f in result.output_files[:3]:  # Show first 3
                print(f"      - {os.path.basename(f)}")
            if len(result.output_files) > 3:
                print(f"      ... and {len(result.output_files) - 3} more")
    
    return processor


def example_batch_process_with_errors():
    """Demonstrate error handling with invalid data"""
    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("BATCH PROCESSING WITH ERRORS")
    print("=" * 80)
    
    import pandas as pd
    
    # Create Excel with some invalid data
    data = [
        # Valid bridge
        ['BRG-001', 'Valid Bridge', 10.0, '25,30,25', 12.0, 15.0, 'RIGHT'],
        # Invalid: span too short
        ['BRG-002', 'Invalid Span', 15.0, '2', 12.0, 15.0, 'RIGHT'],
        # Invalid: negative chainage
        ['BRG-003', 'Bad Chainage', -5.0, '20,20', 9.0, 0.0, 'NONE'],
        # Valid bridge
        ['BRG-004', 'Another Valid', 25.0, '30', 7.5, 0.0, 'NONE'],
        # Invalid: too many spans
        ['BRG-005', 'Too Many Spans', 35.0, '20,' * 25, 12.0, 15.0, 'LEFT'],
    ]
    
    df = pd.DataFrame(data, columns=[
        'bridge_id', 'bridge_name', 'chainage',
        'span_lengths', 'deck_width', 'skew_angle', 'skew_direction'
    ])
    
    excel_file = "demo_with_errors.xlsx"
    df.to_excel(excel_file, sheet_name='BRIDGE_LIST', index=False)
    
    output_dir = "output_with_errors"
    
    config = BatchConfig(
        input_excel_path=excel_file,
        output_base_directory=output_dir,
        max_workers=2,
        skip_invalid=True
    )
    
    processor = BatchProcessor(config)
    processor.run()
    
    print("\nValidation Results:")
    for result in processor.results:
        print(f"  {result.bridge_id}: {result.status.value}")
        if result.error_message:
            print(f"    Error: {result.error_message[:50]}...")
    
    return processor


def example_sequential_vs_parallel():
    """Compare sequential vs parallel processing"""
    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("SEQUENTIAL VS PARALLEL COMPARISON")
    print("=" * 80)
    
    # Create 20 bridge sample
    excel_file = create_sample_batch_file(20, "demo_20_bridges.xlsx")
    output_dir_seq = "output_sequential"
    output_dir_par = "output_parallel"
    
    # Sequential
    print("\n--- Sequential Processing ---")
    config_seq = BatchConfig(
        input_excel_path=excel_file,
        output_base_directory=output_dir_seq,
        use_parallel=False
    )
    
    start = time.time()
    processor_seq = BatchProcessor(config_seq)
    # Mock processing for demo (actual processing would take time)
    print(f"Sequential time: {time.time() - start:.2f}s (simulated)")
    
    # Parallel
    print("\n--- Parallel Processing (4 workers) ---")
    config_par = BatchConfig(
        input_excel_path=excel_file,
        output_base_directory=output_dir_par,
        use_parallel=True,
        max_workers=4
    )
    
    start = time.time()
    processor_par = BatchProcessor(config_par)
    print(f"Parallel time: {time.time() - start:.2f}s (simulated)")
    
    print("\nExpected speedup: ~2-3x with 4 workers (depending on CPU)")


def example_custom_processing():
    """Example of custom processing pipeline"""
    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("CUSTOM PROCESSING PIPELINE")
    print("=" * 80)
    
    # Create sample data
    excel_file = create_sample_batch_file(5, "demo_custom.xlsx")
    
    # Custom config
    config = BatchConfig(
        input_excel_path=excel_file,
        output_base_directory="output_custom",
        max_workers=2,
        organize_by_chainage=False,  # Use bridge ID instead
        create_subfolders=True
    )
    
    processor = BatchProcessor(config)
    
    # Override processing function for custom behavior
    def custom_process(bridge: BridgeInputData):
        """Custom processing - only generate plan view"""
        print(f"  Custom processing: {bridge.bridge_id}")
        
        # Only generate plan view, skip other drawings
        bridge.generate_long_section = False
        bridge.generate_cross_section = False
        bridge.generate_foundation = False
        bridge.generate_reinforcement = False
        
        return processor.process_single_bridge(bridge)
    
    # Read and validate
    bridges = processor.read_excel_input()
    bridges = processor.validate_all(bridges)
    
    # Process with custom function
    results = []
    for bridge in bridges:
        result = custom_process(bridge)
        results.append(result)
    
    processor.results = results
    processor.generate_summary_report()
    
    print(f"\nCustom processing complete: {len(results)} bridges")
    print("Generated: Plan view only (custom setting)")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Run examples
    print("BRIDGE BATCH PROCESSING EXAMPLES")
    print("=" * 80)
    
    # Example 1: Normal batch processing
    processor1 = example_batch_process_10_bridges()
    
    # Example 2: Error handling
    processor2 = example_batch_process_with_errors()
    
    # Example 3: Sequential vs parallel
    example_sequential_vs_parallel()
    
    # Example 4: Custom processing
    example_custom_processing()
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("ALL EXAMPLES COMPLETE")
    print("=" * 80)
```

### File 4: `batch_monitor.py` - Progress Monitoring

```python
"""
Real-time monitoring and progress tracking for batch processing
"""

import time
import threading
from typing import Callable, Optional
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timedelta


@dataclass
class ProgressInfo:
    """Progress information"""
    total: int
    completed: int
    failed: int
    current_bridge: str
    elapsed_time: float
    estimated_remaining: float
    percent_complete: float


class BatchProgressMonitor:
    """
    Real-time progress monitor for batch processing
    """
    
    def __init__(self, total_bridges: int):
        self.total = total_bridges
        self.completed = 0
        self.failed = 0
        self.current_bridge = ""
        self.start_time = time.time()
        self._stop_event = threading.Event()
        self._callback: Optional[Callable[[ProgressInfo], None]] = None
        self._monitor_thread: Optional[threading.Thread] = None
    
    def set_callback(self, callback: Callable[[ProgressInfo], None]):
        """Set callback function for progress updates"""
        self._callback = callback
    
    def update(self, bridge_id: str, status: str):
        """Update progress"""
        self.current_bridge = bridge_id
        
        if status == "COMPLETED":
            self.completed += 1
        elif status == "FAILED":
            self.failed += 1
    
    def get_progress(self) -> ProgressInfo:
        """Get current progress"""
        elapsed = time.time() - self.start_time
        processed = self.completed + self.failed
        
        if processed > 0:
            avg_time = elapsed / processed
            remaining = (self.total - processed) * avg_time
        else:
            remaining = 0
        
        percent = (processed / self.total * 100) if self.total > 0 else 0
        
        return ProgressInfo(
            total=self.total,
            completed=self.completed,
            failed=self.failed,
            current_bridge=self.current_bridge,
            elapsed_time=elapsed,
            estimated_remaining=remaining,
            percent_complete=percent
        )
    
    def start_monitoring(self, interval: float = 1.0):
        """Start monitoring thread"""
        def monitor_loop():
            while not self._stop_event.is_set():
                progress = self.get_progress()
                
                if self._callback:
                    self._callback(progress)
                else:
                    # Default console output
                    self._print_progress(progress)
                
                time.sleep(interval)
        
        self._monitor_thread = threading.Thread(target=monitor_loop)
        self._monitor_thread.start()
    
    def stop_monitoring(self):
        """Stop monitoring"""
        self._stop_event.set()
        if self._monitor_thread:
            self._monitor_thread.join()
    
    def _print_progress(self, progress: ProgressInfo):
        """Print progress to console"""
        bar_length = 40
        filled = int(bar_length * progress.percent_complete / 100)
        bar = '█' * filled + '░' * (bar_length - filled)
        
        eta = timedelta(seconds=int(progress.estimated_remaining))
        elapsed = timedelta(seconds=int(progress.elapsed_time))
        
        print(f"\r[{bar}] {progress.percent_complete:.1f}% | "
              f"{progress.completed}/{progress.total} | "
              f"ETA: {eta} | Current: {progress.current_bridge[:20]:<20}", 
              end='', flush=True)
        
        if progress.percent_complete >= 100:
            print()  # New line when complete


# Example usage
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Simulate batch processing with progress monitoring
    monitor = BatchProgressMonitor(total_bridges=50)
    monitor.start_monitoring(interval=0.5)
    
    # Simulate processing
    for i in range(50):
        time.sleep(0.1)  # Simulate work
        monitor.update(f"BRG-{i+1:03d}", "COMPLETED" if i % 10 != 0 else "FAILED")
    
    monitor.stop_monitoring()
    print("Processing complete!")
```

---

## KEY FEATURES

| Feature | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| **Excel Input** | Flexible column mapping, multiple sheets |
| **Validation** | 10+ validation rules, skip or fix errors |
| **Parallel Processing** | 4-8 workers, 2-3x speedup |
| **Organized Output** | Folders by chainage or bridge ID |
| **Progress Monitoring** | Real-time progress bar and ETA |
| **Error Recovery** | Continue on error, detailed logs |
| **Summary Reports** | Excel summary with statistics |
| **JSON Logs** | Machine-readable processing logs |
| **CLI Interface** | Command-line tool for automation |
| **Custom Pipelines** | Override processing per bridge |

---

## USAGE

### Command Line:
```bash
python batch_processor_core.py -i bridges.xlsx -o output_dir -w 4

# Sequential processing
python batch_processor_core.py -i bridges.xlsx -o output_dir -s

# Continue on errors
python batch_processor_core.py -i bridges.xlsx -o output_dir --continue-on-error
```

### Python API:
```python
from batch_processor_core import BatchProcessor, BatchConfig

config = BatchConfig(
    input_excel_path="100_bridges.xlsx",
    output_base_directory="output",
    max_workers=8,
    use_parallel=True
)

processor = BatchProcessor(config)
processor.run()
```

```
    └── ...
```
